```json
      "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. Use it to find files by pattern (eg. \"src/components/**/*.tsx\"), grep for symbols or keywords (eg. \"API endpoints\"), or answer \"where is X defined / which files reference Y.\" Do NOT use it for code review, design-doc auditing, cross-file consistency checks, or open-ended analysis — it reads excerpts rather than whole files and will miss content past its read window. When calling, specify search breadth: \"quick\" for a single targeted lookup, \"medium\" for moderate exploration, or \"very thorough\" to search across multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks.       "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task.     "type": "skill_listing",
```
```json
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",
```

```text
Now let me compile the findings:

**GPU Status on rtx5090-4x (4x RTX 5090, 32GB each):**
- GPU 0: 47% util, 13645/32607 MiB → PID 90085 → `mvp2_grpo_ced_lr5e6_r1` (GRPO training, step 186/200)
- GPU 1: 84% util, 23195/32607 MiB → PID 122149 → `mvp2_rsft_generate` (RSFT generation, 96/1514, ~3%)


- GPU 2: completely idle with no utilization or memory usage
- GPU 3: 79% util, 10653/32607 MiB → PID 117640 → `mvp2_eval_lr5e6_r1_ckpt100` (eval inference, 50/62, ~81%)

Since there's no constraints file defined, I can't check for explicit GPU allocation rules, but the idle GPU 2 stands out as a clear resource inefficiency with three active experiments running on the other GPUs.

GPU 2 being completely unused isn't a case of allocated-but-not-running — it's simply not assigned to any experiment in the registry, which raises the question of why it's sitting idle. Meanwhile, the GRPO training on GPU 0 is nearly complete at step 186/200, though I'm noticing some concerning spikes in the loss and KL divergence metrics that suggest a potential training instability, even though subsequent steps appear to normalize.

The RSFT generation task is moving much more slowly — only 96 of 1514 batches done, which means roughly 12 more hours at the current pace. The format quality looks solid at 99.6%, but the 9.2% truncation rate and diversity metrics are worth monitoring as this long task continues.

The mean diversity score of 0.9 out of 8 is alarming — it suggests the model is collapsing into nearly identical outputs across generations, which defeats the purpose of having diverse samples to filter from. Even at temperature 0.7, I'd expect much better variation than this.

The eval job is nearly complete at 81% and should wrap up soon. I'm also noticing that massive loss spike in GRPO with a value over 988 trillion — that's clearly a numerical instability issue that needs attention. And without a constraints file, I can't verify whether the GPU usage is staying within any defined boundaries.

Let me pull together the review report now, though I should also double-check the server configuration since bjb2-12179 doesn't appear in the available options.

Actually, I notice the constraints.yaml file hasn't been created yet according to the documentation, so I can't validate GPU boundary compliance. That said, the key issues I'm seeing are the GPU 2 idleness while other experiments are running, the numerical instability spike in GRPO training, and most critically, the RSFT diversity problem — with such low diversity across the 8 generations, the filtering mechanism won't be able to meaningfully differentiate samples over the next 12 hours of runtime. The metric definition is ambiguous, but regardless of the exact interpretation, the diversity score is clearly very low and warrants a warning in my report. I'll flag this as a potential issue without being overly alarmist since I'm uncertain about the precise measurement, then move on to drafting the findings.
```

```text
The worker `worker_check_gpu_and_nli_status_20260515T005927` failed. The worker name suggests it was checking GPU status and NLI status. Since this was a "worker_done" notification with status=failed, I should check what NLI-related experiments exist. The worker itself doesn't appear in the registry as an experiment - it was a worker task, not an experiment.

Let me look at the data more carefully:

Key findings from the registry update:
1. `mvp2_diversity_probe` is now DONE with conclusion=success. It shows unique_triples at T=0.7 is 7/8, T=1.0 is 8/8. 

Actually, I'm seeing something important here - the diversity probe results contradict my earlier concern about RSFT diversity being 0.9/8. The probe measured 7-8 unique triple sets across 3 samples with 8 generations each, while the rsft_generate log was tracking something different like mean Jaccard similarity. These are measuring different aspects of diversity, so I need to reconsider my earlier warning.

Looking at the format_ok_rate too - it was around 50% in the probe due to field name parsing issues, but jumped to 99.6% in the full generation after the fix was applied. The diversity metric discrepancy (0.9/8 vs 7/8 unique) likely reflects the same difference: the probe ran on a small sample before the full pipeline started, while the monitor is tracking 100 documents with potentially different measurement criteria.

The failed worker that was checking GPU and NLI status is harder to diagnose without seeing its actual output. There's no NLI experiment visible in the registry, which suggests the Natural Language Inference component might not be running or accessible - that's probably why the status check failed.

Looking at the RSFT diversity metrics more closely, there's a concerning mismatch: the probe shows 7 out of 8 unique triples, but the full generation monitor reports a mean diversity of 0.9 out of 8. This gap could mean the metrics are measuring different things, the probe's 3 samples weren't representative, or something shifted between the probe and actual generation.

The GRPO learning rate experiment at 5e-6 is showing a troubling pattern - the relative F1 scores have collapsed dramatically from the SFT baseline of 0.4057 down to around 0.007-0.011 across checkpoints 20 through 100, suggesting the training went badly off track.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I understand the diversity metric. Let me look at lines around 320-330 in rsft_generate.py to see the exact computation.

Line 325: `diversity = len(doc_triple_sets) / args.num_generations`

So `doc_triple_sets` is the number of unique triple sets among the 8 generations, divided by 8. If diversity = 0.9/8 ≈ 0.1125, that would mean `len(doc_triple_sets)` ≈ 0.9, which doesn't make sense since len() returns an integer.

Wait, the monitor output says `mean_diversity: 0.9/8`. 

Ah, I see now—the display format on line 260 prints `mean_div` (the average of `len(doc_triple_sets)` across all documents) followed by the total number of generations, so `mean_diversity: 0.9/8` means on average there are 0.9 unique triple-sets per document out of 8 possible generations.

This is puzzling because it suggests some documents produce zero unique triple-sets entirely (all generations fail to parse), while the diversity probe showed 7/8 unique triples at T=0.7—a stark difference that could stem from the probe using fixed code while the running system uses older code, or from measuring diversity differently, or simply from the probe's small sample size of 3 not being representative.

I think the issue might be that `doc_triple_sets` is a set of frozensets, and if parsing is buggy, many outputs can't be converted to triples, leaving empty sets that all count as identical. But the RSFT monitor shows `format_ok_rate: 99.6%`, so format parsing isn't the culprit here. I need to examine the code around line 325 more closely to understand what's actually happening with the triple-set generation.
```

[severity: warning] RSFT diversity 指标矛盾 — 需确认\n\n两个数据源对 T=0.7 生成多样性的结论冲突：\n\n| 来源 | 指标 | 值 | 样本量 |\n|------|------|-----|-------|\n| `mvp2_diversity_probe` (registry) | unique_triples | **7/8** | 3 docs |\n| `mvp2_rsft_generate` (日志 monitor) | mean_diversity | **0.9/8** | 100 docs |\n\nProbe 结论 \"多样性足够，RSFT 可行\"（unique 7-8/8），但实际大规模生成 monitor 显示 diversity 极低（0.9/8）。\n\n可能解释：\n- **指标定义不同**：probe 测 unique triple-sets（结构级去重），monitor 可能测 token-level 或 output-level 相似度\n- **Probe 样本量太小**（仅 3 docs）不代表整体分布\n- **代码修复影响**：probe 之后修复了 evaluator.py（format_ok_rate 50%→99.6%），修复可能影响了 diversity 计算\n\n**建议 Agent 确认 rsft_generate.py 中 `mean_diversity` 的定义**，并对比 probe 和实际生成的测量口径。如果 0.9/8 确实代表\"几乎无多样性\"，当前 12 小时生成任务的产出质量存疑。\n\n### 3. [severity: info] lr5e6 GRPO 轨迹更新：catastrophic forgetting 持续\n\nRegistry 最新数据确认 lr5e6 轨迹全面失败：\n- SFT baseline: rel_f1=**0.4057**, evi_f1=0.7979\n- ckpt-20: rel_f1=0.0077 (−98%)\n- ckpt-40: rel_f1=0.0071\n- ckpt-60: rel_f1=0.0093\n- ckpt-80: rel_f1=0.0116\n- ckpt-100: 评估中（81% 进度）\n\nrel_f1 在 0.007-0.012 区间持续平坦，无恢复信号。evi_f1 从 0.628→0.828 在上升但无法弥补关系抽取能力的完全丧失。ckpt-100 eval 是最后判定点——如果 rel_f1 仍 < 0.05，lr5e6 路线可判定为 negative，需要新方案（如更高 lr + 其他正则化 / RSFT 路线）。",

```json
}
```

```text
Let me also check for the RSFT diversity issue more carefully. The key insight from my analysis:


The diversity_probe shows 7/8 unique triple-sets at T=0.7, suggesting diversity is fine at that level, but the RSFT monitor reports mean_diversity of 0.9/8, indicating low diversity at the output string level. The diversity metric itself measures unique triple-sets rather than string diversity, so there's a discrepancy - the probe found 7/8 unique sets while the monitor shows only 0.9 average unique sets, though the probe was only run on 3 samples so that's a limited sample size.

The RSFT generation process started before the bugfix was applied, and while the bugfix addressed format parsing issues, the monitor shows a 99.6% format_ok_rate, so format parsing doesn't appear to be the bottleneck. The real question is whether the old code computed the diversity metric differently or had a separate bug affecting how it extracted triples - but rather than speculate further, I should focus on whether the RSFT data is still usable or if the generation needs to be rerun.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me analyze the findings:

1. **GPU Status** (unchanged pattern):
   - GPU 0: 56%, 13645 MiB — GRPO training (PID 90085), step 188/200
   - GPU 1: 66%, 23195 MiB — RSFT generate (PID 122149)
   - GPU 2: 0%, 0 MiB — **still idle**
   - GPU 3: 73%, 11243 MiB — eval ckpt-100 (PID 117640)
   
2. **GRPO Training**: The training has progressed to step 188/200 with about 12 steps left, but I'm seeing a concerning pattern of multiple loss and KL divergence spikes rather than isolated incidents — there's one at learning rate 2.9e-6 with extreme values, another at 2.775e-6 with more moderate spikes, plus the earlier spike I noted, suggesting something systematic is going wrong with the training dynamics.

3. **Registry changes**: The `mvp2_diversity_probe` completed successfully with some bugfixes applied.

4. **RSFT bugfix assessment**: The worker addressed issues with the evaluator component.

Now I'm noticing a critical timing issue — the code files were modified at 08:05-08:06, but the running `mvp2_rsft_generate` process (PID 122144) started at 00:09 and loaded the pre-bugfix code at startup. Python modules are loaded when the process begins, so it's still executing the old version. The format_ok_rate looks healthy at 99.6%, but if the bugfix changed how diversity is computed, then the current generation's diversity metric might be inaccurate even though the actual text outputs remain valid.

The key question is whether the RSFT pipeline generates raw outputs first and scores them separately, or if it does inline filtering. If generations.jsonl contains the raw unfiltered text and CED scoring happens in a separate step using the fixed code, then the data should be fine — the monitor's diversity reading is just informational. But if rsft_generate does inline scoring and only saves filtered results, then we have a real problem. I need to check what progress.json actually shows versus what's in generations.jsonl to understand how much data was processed.

Looking at the batch counts, batch 100 out of 1514 with batch_size=2 suggests roughly 200 documents processed, which aligns with the ~188 document IDs in progress.json out of 3027 total. The main things to verify now are whether the bugfix requires restarting RSFT and what's causing those recurring loss/kl spikes in GRPO, plus checking if the eval has finished.
```

```text
Now I have a clearer picture:

1. **GRPO Training (mvp2_grpo_ced_lr5e6_r1)**: At step 188/200, still running. **Multiple loss/kl spikes** observed:
   - One step: loss=988B, kl=70T (catastrophic)
   - Another step (lr=2.9e-6): loss=202K, kl=13.9M
   - Another step (lr=2.775e-6): loss=9.5, kl=685
   These are recurring instabilities, not isolated events. 

This is more concerning than I initially thought. The eval checkpoint at batch 53/62 is nearly done and should wrap up in about 15 minutes, while the RSFT generation has processed around 188 of 3027 documents so far. GPU 2 remains unused, and the diversity probe from the bugfix worker completed successfully with 7 out of 8 unique triple-sets.

Looking at the format parsing discrepancy, the RSFT generation is showing a 99.6% format_ok_rate, which suggests the old code in rsft_generate has a different, likely simpler format validation than what's in evaluator.py. The diversity metric of 0.9/8 is probably counting unique output strings rather than unique triple-sets, so two outputs extracting the same triples but phrased differently would register as distinct. If the diversity calculation is based on the number of unique triple-sets divided by the total generations, then a low diversity score would indicate either the triple extraction itself is broken in the older code or the outputs are genuinely similar.

The critical insight is that the running RSFT is using the old code loaded at 00:09, while the diversity_probe that showed 7/8 unique results used the fixed code. If the bug affected triple extraction or parsing, the old code's triple-sets would be mostly empty, producing that artificially low diversity metric. However, the raw generated text saved to generations.jsonl appears fine. The real question is whether RSFT only saves the raw outputs and defers scoring to later with the fixed code, or if it's already filtering based on the broken triple extraction—that determines whether the generation needs to restart.

Looking at the pipeline structure, scoring and filtering happen after generation as a separate step, so the 1664 raw outputs in generations.jsonl should be valid regardless of the old code's broken metric. The only remaining risk is if rsft_generate.py itself applies filtering during generation based on that broken diversity calculation.

Now I'm reviewing what to report: GRPO shows multiple recurring loss/kl spikes rather than just one isolated spike, suggesting systematic numerical instability that the model keeps recovering from. The RSFT generation is running on pre-bugfix code, but the actual diversity is 7/8 per the diversity_probe while the reported mean_diversity of 0.9/8 is just an artifact of the old triple parser. GPU 2 remains idle with no changes.

Looking at the lr5e6 GRPO trajectory across checkpoints 20 through 80, the rel_f1 scores are catastrophically low (all under 0.012 compared to the SFT baseline of 0.4057), while evi_f1 improved significantly (0.628→0.828). This is textbook reward hacking—the model optimized for evidence formatting at the expense of core relation extraction capability. The ckpt-100 eval is marked as the decisive checkpoint, so I shouldn't jump to conclusions yet, but the flat trajectory through checkpoint 80 is concerning. I should focus my report on the GRPO spike escalation.
```
